# Minimum-wage floors — the fleet's PROPOSAL for the operator's approval

> **NOT LOADED. NOT VERIFIED.** Every figure below was researched by the 48-agent fleet from what it knows; **no official
> source is reachable from this environment**, so nothing has been checked live. This sheet is the approval surface: tick
> a row to approve it; a ticked row is loaded in a separate step, in a dated companion file beside the operator's 114 rows,
> which are never changed. A figure without a named instrument and a date is not a figure — such rows show —.

**Generated 2026-09-11 by `scripts/build-fleet-rate-proposal.mjs` from the fleet run.** 12 AsMs × (2 researchers + 1 voter) + 12 MoT completeness coordinators.

| ☐ | English | AS | United States — American Samoa | USD | — | Yes | Yes | Fair Labor Standards Act §6(a)(3), 29 U.S.C. §206(a)(3), as amended by P.L. 110-28 (2007), P.L. 111-244 (2010), P.L. 112-149 (2012) and P.L. 114-61 (2015) — special industry minimum wages for American Samoa; rates in 29 C.F.R. Part 697 · 2024-09-30 | — | low | A=B | A and B agree: rate null, no single rate. American Samoa is the only U.S. jurisdiction where the $7.25 FLSA floor does not apply as a single rate and there is no local minimum-wage statute. Federal law sets special minimum wages BY INDUSTRY (about 18 classifications — fish canning/processing, government, retail/wholesale, shipping/transportation, tourism, construction, etc.) under FLSA §6(a)(3). P.L. 114-61 (2015) raises every industry rate by $0.40 on 30 September every third year (2015, 2018, 2021, 2024, next 2027) until each reaches $7.25, where it caps. After the 30 Sep 2024 step the industry rates cluster roughly between $6 and $7.25 (shipping/transportation and government at or near $7.25; fish canning in the low-to-mid $6 range), but neither researcher can cite an individual industry figure with confidence, so rate is null; USDOL WHD publishes the schedule (29 C.F.R. Part 697). English and Samoan co-official; English is the language of the federal statute. URL not fetched from this environment. |

| ☐ | Sinhala | LK | Sri Lanka | LKR | 105.000 | Yes | No | National Minimum Wage of Workers Act No. 3 of 2016, as amended by Act No. 16 of 2021, Act No. 25 of 2024 and the 2025 Amendment Act · 2025 (Rs 21,000/840); 2024 step Rs 17,500/700 held with higher confidence | daily 840 ÷ 8 h = 105.000 (monthly 21,000 ÷ 25 d = 840; monthly ÷ 173.33 would give 121.16) | medium | A=B | A=B. National Minimum Wage of Workers Act No. 3 of 2016 sets a single national floor for all workers in any industry/service (originally Rs 10,000/month, Rs 400/day), amended by Act No. 16 of 2021 (Rs 12,500 / Rs 500), by Act No. 25 of 2024 (Rs 17,500/month, Rs 700/day, absorbing the budgetary relief allowance; A recalls effective 1 January 2024), and — implementing Budget 2025 — by a 2025 Amendment Act to Rs 21,000/month and Rs 840/day (both agents recall passage in 2025; exact Act number and commencement to verify). If the 2025 amendment is not yet in force the operative figures are Rs 17,500 / Rs 700 (= 87.50/h). The statute publishes both monthly and daily; the daily rate is for an 8-hour working day (Wages Boards Ordinance / Shop and Office Employees Act, 8 h/day, 45–48 h/week), so hourly = 840 ÷ 8; monthly 21,000 = 25 d × 840. Sectoral Wages Boards set higher trade-specific minima above the floor; Budget Relief Allowance Acts historically added fixed allowances. Monthly ÷ 173.33 would give 121.16 — not used, statute defines the day. |

| ☐ | Dhivehi | MV | Maldives | MVR | 38.462 | Yes | Yes | Employment Act (Law No. 2/2008) Chapter 8 as amended by Act 22/2020; Minimum Wage Order (Ministry of Economic Development, 8 Nov 2021, effective 1 Jan 2022) · 1 January 2022 (possible 2024–25 revision unverified) | monthly 8,000 ÷ 208 h (48 h/wk × 52 ÷ 12, as used in the Order) = 38.462 — matches the Order's own published hourly 38.46 | medium | A=B | A=B. First statutory minimum wage under Chapter 8 of the Employment Act (Law No. 2/2008) as added by the 6th Amendment (Act 22/2020); Minimum Wage Order issued 8 Nov 2021 by the Ministry of Economic Development, effective 1 January 2022. Three tiers by employer size, hence no single rate: small enterprises MVR 4,500/month (published hourly 21.63); medium MVR 7,000 (33.65/h); large enterprises and public sector MVR 8,000 (38.46/h). The Order itself publishes the hourly figures on a 48-hour week (208 h/month), so no 173.33 conversion is applied. Applied initially to Maldivian citizens only; extension to expatriate workers scheduled later. Both agents recall a revision under discussion / possibly effective 2024–25 with higher tiers but cannot confirm figures — treat 2022 rates as possibly superseded. Rate shown = top tier (large enterprise/public sector). |

No single national minimum wage. Bangladesh Labour Act 2006 (Act XLII of 2006), Chapter XI ss. 138–149, creates a Minimum Wages Board that recommends sector-by-sector floors (40+ sectors: garments, tea, tannery, pharmaceuticals, rice mills, etc.), each gazetted separately by the Ministry of Labour and Employment. Section 140A (2013 amendment) permits a national minimum wage declaration; A recalls a very low all-sector floor (c. Tk 1,500/month, 2015) but is not confident it was ever gazetted as binding. Statutory hours (ss. 100–102): 8 h/day, 48 h/week → 208 h/month. Best-known sector floor is ready-made garments Tk 12,500/month (Grade 5 helper) effective 1 December 2023 with 5% annual increment — see the separate RMG sector row. ## Coordinators' completeness notes

- **Aset** (Δ): Coverage: all 7 region_members (US federal floor, DC, PR, GU, VI, AS, MP) have exactly one row each; no duplicates, no orphan rows. Against the region as defined (DC + the five inhabited territories), nothing is missing. The U.S. Minor Outlying Islands (UM) are the only other U.S. insular area; they have no permanent population and no minimum-wage regime, so their absence is correct, not a gap — but the package note should say so explicitly so a reader does not mistake silence for omission. Instrument quality: 6 of 7 rows carry a specific, verifiable instrument (US: 29 U.S.C. §206(a)(1)(C) + P.L. 110-28; DC: D.C. Code §32-1003 + D.C. Law 21-144 + Initiative 82; PR: Ley 47-2021 + Decreto Mandatorio 2024; VI: 24 V.I.C. §4 + Act 7856; AS: 29 U.S.C. §206(a)(3) + 29 C.F.R. Part 697 with the four amending public laws; MP: §206(a)(1)(C) via P.L. 110-28 §8103, P.L. 111-244, P.L. 113-34, 4 CMC §9211). The Guam row is the sole defect: the field itself embeds an A-vs-B dispute over the enacting public law (P.L. 34-06 vs P.L. 35-38) and the as_of is degraded to a bare year — flag for operator confirmation with Guam DOL before publication. Minor: the Puerto Rico row's 'Decreto Mandatorio (2024)' has no decree number; acceptable but could be tightened. Federal-vs-local naming (the region brief's requirement) is satisfied on every row: DC/PR/GU/VI name both the $7.25 floor and the higher local statute; MP names both and explains the local act is superseded in effect; AS names the federal §6(a)(3) industry scheme and the absence of a local statute. · rows without an instrument: United States — Guam (GU): statutory_instrument names 22 GCA §3105 but leaves the amending public law unresolved in the field itself ('amending public law disputed: A says P.L. 34-06, B says P.L. 35-38') — a citation that contains a disagreement is not a citation; the enacting law and its effective date must be pinned before this row is usable
- **Asar** (Δ): Coverage: all 38 region_members have a row (52 rows total: 14 Canada = federal + 10 provinces + 3 territories; 2 Mexico = general + ZLFN; 7 Central America; 13 sovereign Caribbean states; 2 US territories; 6 UK OTs; 4 Dutch; 4 French). Statutory-instrument quality: 15 rows flagged above; of these, IO is 'None' by design and 7 enabling-statute-only rows (DJ, ER, ET, RW, SO, SS, UG) are NOT flagged because citing the enabling act with 'no order issued' is the correct, specific answer. The recurring defect is unnumbered subordinate instruments (Legal Notices, SIs, GNs, decrees) — KM and MZ are the two where even the instrument's identity is unresolved. Beyond scope but noted: ZA row's own note suggests the A/B figures are consecutive annual steps (2025 = 28.79, 2026 = 30.23), which the operator should verify against the March 2026 gazette; KE's 225.33 h divisor departs from the 173.33 h convention used elsewhere and should be flagged in the cross-region consistency pass. · rows without an instrument: KM — 'Loi n° 12-0xx/AU — number unverified' is a placeholder, and the implementing SMIG decree is unnamed/undated; instrument effectively unidentified, IO — statutory_instrument is 'None'; justified (no resident workforce, no ordinance) but the field is empty in substance, SD — 'Council of Ministers minimum-wage resolution (2020)' carries no resolution number or date; Labour Act 1997 cited without section, MZ — instrument type itself unresolved (Council of Ministers decree vs Ministerial Diploma); no number or year for the annual table, SZ — 'sectoral Wages Regulation Orders (Legal Notices)' names no Legal Notice number or year for any sector, LS — 'annual Legal Notice' with no LN number or year; parent act also uncertain (Labour Code Order 1992 vs Labour Act 2024), MG — 'Décret annuel' with no decree number or year, ZM — 'Statutory Instruments 2023' with no SI numbers for the three orders, BW — 'Minimum Wages Order (Statutory Instrument), 2023 revision' with no SI number, SC — 'Employment (National Minimum Wage) Regulations (S.I., as amended)' with no SI number or amendment year, MW — 'Order / Government Notice' with no GN number; even the instrument name is hedged, AO — 'reported 2024 successor decree' unnamed; only the superseded Decreto Presidencial 54/22 is identified, KE — Regulation of Wages (General) (Amendment) Order 2024 is named but the Legal Notice number is absent, NA — National Minimum Wage Order cited as 'Government Notice, Government Gazette 2024' with no GN or Gazette number, ZA — annual adjustment 'Government Gazette notice' with no GN/Gazette number for the 2025 (or 2026) step
- **Pangu** (Σ): Coverage against region_members is complete: all eight South Asian sovereign states (AF, BD, BT, IN, MV, NP, PK, LK) have a national row, and no other sovereign state belongs to this region. Sub-national/sector rows are present for IN (Delhi, West Bengal, Punjab), PK (ICT, Punjab, Sindh) and BD (RMG). Observations for the operator, not rate research: (1) Pakistan is fully provincial since the 18th Amendment, and the package names the Khyber Pakhtunkhwa Minimum Wages Act 2013 and Balochistan Minimum Wages Act 2021 in the national row but produces no KP or Balochistan row — if the PK state pattern is meant to be complete the way the IN pattern is sampled, those two are gaps; if only a sample is intended, say so explicitly in the PK national row. (2) No statutory_instrument field is literally empty, but ten rows cite an instrument by description rather than by number/date; the weakest are West Bengal and Punjab (India), which name only 'notifications' with no identifiable act of publication. (3) Six of the fifteen rows leave rate null despite both agents holding the same monthly wage (Delhi, ICT, PK-Punjab, Sindh) or a last-confirmed figure (Bhutan); these nulls are policy holds (divisor choice, revision choice), not missing data — the operator's divisor ruling for Pakistan would resolve three rows at once. (4) Every source_url is unfetched; none has been verified in this package. · rows without an instrument: Afghanistan — the only concrete instrument is the 2007 Labour Law; the 'presidential decree raising the government rate (c. 2015)' carries no decree number or gazette issue, and the row concedes no post-2021 instrument is known, so the cited basis is not shown to be operative, India — Delhi — 'DA-linked minimum wage revision notification (Oct 2024 per A; Apr 2025 per B)': no notification number or date, and the row has not settled which revision is the instrument, India — West Bengal — 'West Bengal Labour Department minimum wage notifications': generic, no notification number, date or scheduled employment named, India — Punjab — 'Labour Commissioner, Punjab, minimum wage notifications': generic, no notification number, date or scheduled employment named, Pakistan — Islamabad Capital Territory — instrument hedged between the 1969 Ordinance and the 1961 Ordinance 'as applied to ICT'; the Islamabad Minimum Wages Board notification is unnumbered and undated, Pakistan — Punjab — 'Punjab Minimum Wages Board notification following Punjab Budget 2025-26': notification number and date absent, Pakistan — Sindh — 'Sindh Minimum Wages Board notification following Sindh Budget 2025-26': notification number and date absent, and A holds the figure itself with low confidence, Bhutan — 'Minimum Wage notification/executive order (MoLHR, 2014; MoICE thereafter)': no order number or date, and the row itself says the 2014 order is likely superseded by an unidentified 2023-24 instrument, Sri Lanka — 'the 2025 Amendment Act': Act number and commencement date missing for the very step the reported rate (840/day) depends on, Bangladesh — Garment (RMG) sector — the Nov 2023 MoLE gazette notification is cited without its SRO/notification number
- **Sofia** (Σ): Completeness check, pod Σ East Asia. All 8 region_members have a row (CN, HK, MO, TW, JP, KR, KP, MN), plus 4 sub-national rows (Beijing, Shanghai, Shenzhen, Tokyo) that the region brief asked for. Against my own list of sovereign/de-facto jurisdictions in East Asia — China, Japan, South Korea, North Korea, Mongolia, Taiwan, and the two SARs — nothing is missing; there is no additional sovereign state in this region. No row has an empty statutory_instrument; every row names at least the enabling Act. The five rows listed are flagged for vagueness only in the sense that the rate-setting instrument (notice/announcement/resolution) is cited by type and approximate year without a document number or exact date, which matters most for Beijing where the edition is itself the disputed point. Not flagged: CN national (Order No. 21 of 2004 named), Shanghai (2023 notice named, though also without number), HK (Cap. 608 + 2025 Amendment of Schedule 3 Notice — precise), JP national and Tokyo (Act No. 137 of 1959 + FY2025 bureau decision), KR (Act No. 3927 + MOEL public notice), MO (Lei n.º 5/2020 — precise). Rates were not researched or checked, per my role. · rows without an instrument: China — Beijing (instrument names the notice type but not the edition: 'Notice … on Adjusting the Municipal Minimum Wage Standard — 2025 edition per A, 2023 edition per B'; no document number or date, and the edition itself is disputed), China — Shenzhen (Guangdong notice cited as 粤府函 [2021] with no document number; Shenzhen HRSS notice unnamed), Taiwan (2026 rate cited as 'Ministry of Labor announcement of the 2026 minimum wage (Sept 2025)' with no announcement number or date), Mongolia (Tripartite Committee resolution cited as '(2023)' with no resolution number), North Korea (instrument is the Socialist Labour Law, which sets wage-scale principles; row explicitly states no minimum-wage instrument exists — acceptable as a documented absence, not a vague citation)
- **Thoth** (Σ): Sovereign-state coverage is complete: all eleven Southeast Asian states (10 ASEAN members + Timor-Leste) have a row, and no other sovereign state exists in the region. The only gaps against region_members are the two Australian external territories (CX, CC), which the package delegates to an AU row it does not contain — acceptable only if the operator's AU row actually exists and is marked as covering them. No row has an empty statutory_instrument, but five (ID, KH, LA, BN, TL) cite an instrument without a verifiable number or with an admitted dispute; MM, TH, PH, VN, MY and SG are adequately pinned (SG structurally, as a no-minimum-wage jurisdiction). Vietnam's regional-pattern requirement is met by the four-region table in notes; Indonesia, Philippines and Thailand each name their sub-national pattern. Every citation in the package is from memory and unfetched, per its own admission. · rows without an instrument: ID — instrument cites 'Ministry of Manpower regulation on 2026 minimum-wage setting (late 2025)' and 'Keputusan Gubernur DKI Jakarta on UMP Tahun 2026' with no regulation number and no governor's decree number (notes admit 'not verifiable from here'), KH — 'annual MLVT Prakas ... for 2026 (issued Sept/Oct 2025)' carries no Prakas number; notes say 'confirm the Prakas number', LA — the operative instrument for the recalled LAK 2,500,000 rate is 'later 2024/2025 notice unverified'; only the superseded 2023 PMO Notice No. 1502 is cited, BN — 'Minimum Wage Order (sectoral ... 2023)' explicitly marked 'citation and effective date unverified', TL — Government Decree number disputed (9/2012 vs 29/2012); the load-bearing instrument is not pinned
- **Thor** (Σ): REGION COMPLETENESS: all 79 region_members have a row (cc-by-cc match, including NF/GU/MP/AS/NC/PF/WF territory keys and all 14 Pacific UN members AU NZ PG FJ SB VU WS TO KI FM MH PW NR TV). Nothing in the named Oceania/Pacific list is missing. UN SWEEP: the voter's note names 25 states with no owner (15 Africa, 10 Europe). My own pass over the 193 finds a further 20 UN members that appear neither in this pod's rows nor in the voter's sweep note (TL CV GQ RW MZ AO SD NP BN KP SM LI MD BY KG KZ CU HT SR YE SY, plus the commonly held DZ AO CM CD CI EG KE ML MA NG SN TZ UG which I assume the Africa/MENA regions own); the voter says these are 'in the operator's record' but does not enumerate that record, so the merge must confirm each rather than trust the assertion — SM and LI are the sharpest gaps because this pod explicitly swept European microstates (AD, MC) and skipped them; KG and KZ likewise for Central Asia (UZ/TJ/TM swept, KG/KZ not); SR for Guyana's neighbour. The 'missing' list therefore has two tiers: the 25 the voter itself flagged as ownerless, and 20 'confirm-held' entries that are silently missing if no other region claims them. INSTRUMENT QUALITY: 30 of 79 rows cite an instrument with no number, year or gazette reference (listed). Rows citing 'None' with a statute that removes or never created a floor (TO, TK, PN, IS, ET, SO, ER, DJ, SS) are acceptable; NR and NU say 'None known', which is a statement about the researcher, not the law. Separately, 15 rows carry source_url '—' (TK PN KM LA AF TM SO ER DJ SS GM GW ST MR LY) and every URL in the package is 'as known; unfetched' — no source was verified.
